# Geometry Bridge: Mathematics Attack Plan

## Workstream Metadata

- Kind: `priority`
- Rank: `proposed`
- Value: `unscored`
- Cost: `unscored`

## Scope

This priority folder owns the focused mathematics and geometry attack plan for moving from the Master Equation of Motion to emergent assembly geometry without jumping directly to full observer-level GR. The workstream exists because the current stack has strong pieces in the Master EOM, Noether swarm architecture, nested shell swarm geometry, and emergent metric map, but it lacks one controlled intermediate object:

$$
\text{Master EOM}
\longrightarrow
\text{branch-local assembly response}
\longrightarrow
\text{Noether sea / observer-geometry export}.
$$

The central target is a branch-local response theorem. For a candidate assembly branch $B$, define a retained state

$$
z_B
=
\left(
Y,\nu,\eta,J,\Lambda,\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{hist}},
Q_{ab},\mathcal{Z}^{ab},\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{sea}}^{ab}
\right),
$$

and a local environment or Noether sea record $\theta$. The first bridge object is a residual equation

$$
\mathcal{R}_B(z_B;\theta)=0
$$

on a root-regular, ledger-consistent branch chart. The first variation is

$$
D_z\mathcal{R}_B[\delta z]
+
D_\theta\mathcal{R}_B[\delta\theta]
=0.
$$

After quotienting gauge directions and fixing the root-ledger convention, a valid response row has the form

$$
\delta z
=
-
\mathcal{G}_B
D_\theta\mathcal{R}_B[\delta\theta],
$$

where $\mathcal{G}_B$ is the inverse or bordered generalized inverse of $D_z\mathcal{R}_B$ on the retained non-gauge subspace.

The output is not a metric yet. The output is the geometry-export packet

$$
\mathcal{K}_{B}^{\mathrm{geom}}
:
\delta\theta
\mapsto
\left(
\delta\ln T_B,\,
\delta Q_{ab},\,
\delta\mathcal{Z}^{ab},\,
\delta\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{sea}}^{ab},\,
\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{pf}},\,
\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{ledger}}
\right).
$$

Only after this packet is branch-certified should [Emergent Metric](../../../content/markdown/aaa/spacetime/emergent-metric.md) consume it as candidate input for $N$, $u^i_{\text{sea}}$, $e^a{}_i$, and $\gamma_{ij}$.

## Core Theory Advance

The first actual mathematical advance of this bucket is the **branch-response reduction**:

$$
\text{emergent geometry recovery}
\quad\leadsto\quad
\text{a gauge-quotiented response problem on one retained assembly branch}.
$$

Instead of asking immediately for a full effective metric, fix one root-regular branch chart and treat the surrounding Noether sea or assembly environment $\theta$ as a perturbing record. The branch response is obtained from the linearized equation

$$
D_z\mathcal{R}_B[\delta z]
=
-
D_\theta\mathcal{R}_B[\delta\theta].
$$

Let $\mathfrak g_B$ be the finite-dimensional or finite-mode gauge space generated by translations, rotations, phase shifts, period choice, root-label relabeling, and any declared branch-family neutral direction. Let $\Pi_B^\perp$ project onto a chosen complement. If

$$
\Pi_B^\perp D_z\mathcal{R}_B\Pi_B^\perp
:
\Pi_B^\perp T_{z_B}\mathcal Z_B
\longrightarrow
\Pi_B^\perp\mathcal F_B
$$

has a bounded inverse $\mathcal{G}_B$ on the retained root stratum, then the non-gauge first-order assembly response is

$$
\boxed{
\delta z_\perp
=
-
\mathcal{G}_B
\Pi_B^\perp D_\theta\mathcal{R}_B[\delta\theta].
}
$$

This is stronger than another checklist item. It turns the geometry problem into a computable derivative problem. The branch chart either supplies $\mathcal{G}_B$ and therefore a local geometry response, or it fails with a mathematical obstruction: non-invertible quotient derivative, root-stratum failure, missing response variable, or ledger mismatch.

The first observer-facing check can also be stated before full PPN recovery. If the geometry export gives a weak local lapse perturbation $\delta N$ and a spatial-compliance perturbation $\delta\gamma_{ij}$ in a local homogeneous Noether sea frame, then the isotropic weak-field equality between dynamical and lensing potentials has the branch-response form

$$
\boxed{
\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{Weyl=dyn},B}
=
\delta N
+
\frac{1}{6}h^{ij}\delta\gamma_{ij}
=0.
}
$$

This follows from the comparison forms

$$
N=1+\frac{\Phi_{\mathrm{dyn}}}{c_0^2}+O(c_0^{-4}),
\qquad
\gamma_{ij}
=
\left(
1-\frac{2\Psi_{\mathrm{sp}}}{c_0^2}
\right)h_{ij}
+O(c_0^{-4}),
$$

for which $h^{ij}\delta\gamma_{ij}=-6\Psi_{\mathrm{sp}}/c_0^2$. Thus $\Psi_{\mathrm{sp}}=\Phi_{\mathrm{dyn}}$ is equivalent to the displayed residual vanishing. This condition is not yet a GR proof, but it is a concrete branch-response equation that can reject a lapse-only geometry bridge before the wider metric program is attempted.

## Priority List

1. `branch_local_response_object` — Define the exact residual object $\mathcal{R}_B(z_B;\theta)$ for one diagnostic branch chart, including root variables, bounded speed factors if used, branch labels, energy/action rows, shape tensor, exposure tensor, and Noether sea response tensor. Status: `staged`; packet: [branch-local-response-object](branch-local-response-object.md). Depends on: none.

2. `root_sensitive_linearization` — Derive the root-sensitive first variation of $\mathcal{R}_B$, including $D\eta$, $DJ$, delayed force variation, support variation, speed-factor variation, and event-endpoint variation. Status: `staged`; packet: [branch-local-response-object](branch-local-response-object.md). Depends on: `branch_local_response_object`.

3. `gauge_quotient_and_bordered_inverse` — Identify translation, rotation, phase, period, root-label, and branch-family gauge directions, then define the bordered inverse or generalized inverse $\mathcal{G}_B$ used in the response equation. Status: `staged`; packet: [branch-local-response-object](branch-local-response-object.md). Depends on: `root_sensitive_linearization`.

4. `geometry_export_packet` — Compute the branch-local geometry export $\mathcal{K}_{B}^{\mathrm{geom}}$, with first rows $\delta\ln T_B$, $\delta Q_{ab}$, $\delta\mathcal{Z}^{ab}$, $\delta\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{sea}}^{ab}$, preferred-frame leakage, and ledger mismatch residuals. Status: `staged`; packet: [geometry-export-and-interface-variation](geometry-export-and-interface-variation.md). Depends on: `gauge_quotient_and_bordered_inverse`.

5. `assembly_interface_variation` — Apply the response packet to the assembly-Noether sea interface diagnostic $D_{a,X}(\mathbf{x},t)$ and derive the first variation of the level set $\partial\Omega_a(D_X,t)$. Status: `staged`; packet: [geometry-export-and-interface-variation](geometry-export-and-interface-variation.md). Depends on: `geometry_export_packet`.

6. `virtual_work_curl_test` — Test whether the branch-local delayed-force response is action-compatible by computing the finite-mode virtual-work curl $\mathcal{C}_{pq}=\partial_pW_q-\partial_qW_p$ on the same retained root stratum. Status: `staged`; packet: [action-curl-and-adm-projection](action-curl-and-adm-projection.md). Depends on: `root_sensitive_linearization`.

7. `adm_cartan_projection` — Translate a passed geometry-export packet into the ADM/Cartan observer fields $N$, $u^i_{\text{sea}}$, $e^a{}_i$, and $\gamma_{ij}$ as a constitutive projection, not as substrate curvature. Status: `single-mode-axial-cancellation-closed`; packets: [action-curl-and-adm-projection](action-curl-and-adm-projection.md), [axial-response-adm-projection](axial-response-adm-projection.md), [axial-projection-coefficient-ratio](axial-projection-coefficient-ratio.md), [axial-medium-response-realization](axial-medium-response-realization.md), [axial-tensor-coefficient-extraction](axial-tensor-coefficient-extraction.md), [specific-branch-medium-response-row](specific-branch-medium-response-row.md), [retained-branch-medium-response-candidate](retained-branch-medium-response-candidate.md), [retained-medium-action-ledger-row](retained-medium-action-ledger-row.md), [retained-medium-response-object-emission](retained-medium-response-object-emission.md), [octahedral-exposure-derivative-adjoint](octahedral-exposure-derivative-adjoint.md), [octahedral-affine-exposure-matrix](octahedral-affine-exposure-matrix.md), and [octahedral-full-coordinate-exposure-matrix](octahedral-full-coordinate-exposure-matrix.md). Depends on: `geometry_export_packet` and `virtual_work_curl_test`.

8. `minimal_worked_branch` — Choose the smallest available branch chart, likely the current exact-antipodal $M=3$ shell swarm row or the no-fixed-speed-window source-atlas successor, and populate the response packet far enough to classify it as `response-passed`, `response-open`, or `response-rejected`.    Latest status update: `h39-evaluator-source-certificates-to-component-derived-primitive-vector-closure`; latest packets: [octahedral-fold-aware-cross-binary-theta3minus-speed-dependent-fold-pair-first-y-gd-root-tangent-cauchy-majorant-tail-lemma](octahedral-fold-aware-cross-binary-theta3minus-speed-dependent-fold-pair-first-y-gd-root-tangent-cauchy-majorant-tail-lemma.md), [octahedral-fold-aware-cross-binary-theta3minus-speed-dependent-fold-pair-first-y-gd-h39-shared-domain-coefficient-series-engine](octahedral-fold-aware-cross-binary-theta3minus-speed-dependent-fold-pair-first-y-gd-h39-shared-domain-coefficient-series-engine.md), and [octahedral-fold-aware-cross-binary-theta3minus-speed-dependent-fold-pair-first-y-gd-h39-shared-domain-primitive-diagnostic](octahedral-fold-aware-cross-binary-theta3minus-speed-dependent-fold-pair-first-y-gd-h39-shared-domain-primitive-diagnostic.md). The evaluator now derives candidate source and removable-Jacobian Cauchy outer bounds from the centered branch coordinate seminorms, derives a candidate $N_G$ Cauchy outer bound only from complete branch denominator Cauchy data, threads those inputs into candidate $E_R$, $M_R$, $M_G$, and $\nu_J$ analytic-remainder profiles, reports the $N_G$ branch-sum budget margin against the h39-implied $M_G$ ceiling, emits a full Cauchy primitive profile-vector status for $\mathfrak P_{39}^{\mathrm{cand}}=(E_R,M_R,M_G,\nu_J,L_J)$ that distinguishes missing components, complete-but-scale-open vectors, and complete vectors that close the h39 profile replay, packages the resulting candidate vector into a primitive-vector backend artifact for the existing h39 primitive diagnostic, routes that artifact through a promotion-theorem bridge that names the exact obstruction, adds a same-domain provenance verifier that can promote only the h39 primitive continuous-tail row when $E_R$, $M_R$, $M_G$, $\nu_J$, $L_J$, $\rho_X$, and $r_X$ have matching directed-rounded graph-centered provenance and the Rouché replay closes, and now emits the finite branch-coordinate witness handoff for the $K_\varepsilon$ majorant: $(\nu_-,D_\varepsilon,\Phi_\varepsilon,B_\nu,S_{\delta,\varepsilon},S_{\phi,\varepsilon};\mathfrak S)$ on both branches, with $S_{\delta,\varepsilon}$ and $S_{\phi,\varepsilon}$ certified by positive-Taylor/geometric-tail $\sinh$ envelopes rather than a bare transcendental flag. The primitive diagnostic's upstream-source composition now consumes the evaluator's branch-coordinate witness-set artifact directly when no external $M_K$ witness is supplied: it replays $K_\varepsilon$, extracts the generated $M_K$ witness, feeds $L_J=\rho^{41}M_K$ through the existing $L_J$ subset, and only then composes the seven primitive components. The same upstream composer now also consumes one shared-domain evaluator artifact directly as the source handoff for coordinate-Cauchy and denominator-Cauchy witnesses: it aggregates the evaluator's per-branch $R_{\varepsilon,43}$ and Jacobian profiles into $E_R,M_R,\nu_J$, aggregates the $N_G$ profile into $M_G$, verifies evaluator-emitted source certificates when the structural and positive-Taylor transcendental-envelope predicates close, and refuses to auto-consume complete-but-scale-open evaluator primitive vectors as reducer inputs. The component-subset composition now synthesizes the primitive-vector backend from the seven certified component proofs after reducer preflight, so the reducer-safe evaluator row closes without manual source wrapping or an external $M_K$ wrapper: with $R_R=R_J=0.9$, $R_{J,\mathrm{num}}=1.35$, $R_G=0.5$, $\rho_X=0.01$, and $r_X=0.008$, the real evaluator-derived profile values replay through evaluator-emitted source certificates, the evaluator-emitted $K_\varepsilon$ witness set, and a graph-radii witness to close the h39 primitive continuous-tail row. This still does not retain the branch and still does not close full `I1` complement closure because the $\theta_{3-}^{-}$ fold collar still requires the full first-y quotient enclosure, scaled remainder, `I1` composition, quadrature, and retained branch status. The regular-root side has a directed-rounded sheet-cover and a hybrid raw-$F$/fold-$p$ complement-slab closure.

   Current H39 refinement: the graph-radii witness is now emitted by the shared-domain evaluator when $\rho_X,r_X$ are declared on the same signature and is also exposed at the coefficient-artifact boundary. The reducer-safe row therefore closes from one evaluator artifact carrying source certificates, the $K_\varepsilon$ witness set, and the graph-radii witness; no manual source wrapper, external $M_K$ wrapper, or separate graph-radii object is needed for that seven-input primitive continuous-tail replay. The same source-composition route now also consumes full shared-domain coefficient artifacts. For one emitted `h39_coefficient_cell`, the diagnostic unwraps that cell directly. For a multi-row artifact, it derives a same-domain, same-radius aggregate coordinate-Cauchy source by per-branch maxima, selects one whole denominator-Cauchy source row with maximal certified $N_G$ outer bound rather than forming a synthetic branch-sum source, reduces $K_\varepsilon$ back to one certified witness per branch before the max-over-branches replay, uses the artifact-level graph-radii witness, and synthesizes the primitive-vector backend only from the seven certified component proofs. It still refuses fixed speed-band fields, rejects radii drift as an `open-provenance-value-mismatch`, and keeps mixed-Cauchy-radius multi-row artifacts open.

   Current H39 closure-sprint refinement: the primitive diagnostic now emits an explicit `certified_seven_input_primitive_witness` object for the same-domain vector $(E_R,M_R,M_G,\nu_J,L_J,\rho_X,r_X;\mathfrak S)$ whenever the seven component proofs and reducer replay certify the h39 continuous-tail row. This changes the status from "component proofs imply a primitive row" to a durable witness artifact with component values, witness families, source provenance, graph-radii values, and reducer replay recorded in one place. The full live h38-to-h39 coefficient artifact is not yet closed: at the current wide coordinate-source radii, the optional coordinate-Cauchy source certificate hits `sinh Taylor majorant overflowed before the tail bound`. The evaluator now records that as a `source_certificate_obstructions` entry instead of aborting, so the next mathematical blocker is to tighten or partition the coordinate source envelope enough for the same-domain source certificates to exist on the full h38 cover.

   Latest H39 coordinate-source envelope refinement: the evaluator now accepts an ordered list of same-domain coordinate source envelope candidates and selects the first candidate whose coordinate-Cauchy source certificate computes, recording failed wider candidates as envelope-selection diagnostics rather than as a successful source handoff. A full h38-cover probe shows that the overflow was an envelope-size obstruction, not a missing branch witness: uniform radii $R_R=R_J=0.01$, $R_{J,\mathrm{num}}=0.02$, and $R_G=0.01$ validate with `source_certificate_obstruction_count=0` and an input-ready primitive backend. The same probe also exposes the sharper closure blocker. At $R_R=0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1,0.15$, source certification succeeds but the h39 primitive profile vector remains scale-open; at $R_R\ge0.2$ the coordinate $\sinh$ majorant overflows before certification. The live mathematical target is therefore no longer just "avoid overflow." ## Immediate Next Packet

The specific rigid octahedral source-only row is now closed as a decision packet. The retained metric-cancellation route now has a candidate response theorem in [retained-branch-medium-response-candidate](retained-branch-medium-response-candidate.md): an isotropic linear trace-free medium response must have the form

$$
\Pi_{\mathrm{tf}}\delta\mathcal M_{\mathrm{sea}}^{ab}
=
\kappa_Z\delta\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{oct,tf}}^{ab},
\qquad
\kappa_Z=-r_{ZM}.
$$

The retained metric-cancellation route now has a derivative-adjoint closure for the isotropic axial octahedral probe in [octahedral-exposure-derivative-adjoint](octahedral-exposure-derivative-adjoint.md):

$$
D_\varepsilon Z_{\mathrm{oct}}^{ab}
=
\zeta_{\delta Z}A^{ab},
\qquad
D_\varepsilon Z_{\mathrm{oct}}^{*}(U)
=
\zeta_{\delta Z}A_{ab}U^{ab}.
$$

The fixed-ledger affine probe closure in [octahedral-affine-exposure-matrix](octahedral-affine-exposure-matrix.md) now extends that column to

$$
D_HZ_{\mathrm{oct}}
:
\operatorname{End}(\mathbb R^3)
\longrightarrow
\mathrm{Sym}_0^2(h),
$$

with an explicit adjoint, trace/shear/rotation decomposition, and a passing executable diagnostic in [octahedral-affine-exposure-probe.mjs](../../../scripts/neutral-swarm/octahedral-affine-exposure-probe.mjs). Together with the completed-square response block, this closes first-order axial observer cancellation for the single isotropic octahedral perturbation when $\lambda_M\ne0$ and $r_{ZM}=\lambda_Z/\lambda_M$, and it also closes the affine shear probe on the same certified root ledger.

The same-ledger affine branch-coordinate derivative in [octahedral-full-coordinate-exposure-matrix](octahedral-full-coordinate-exposure-matrix.md) now closes

$$
D_H^{\mathrm{coord}}Z_{\mathrm{oct}},
$$

including delayed endpoint transport, tangent transport, curvature, and $J_\rho$ variation. Its executable diagnostic in [octahedral-coordinate-exposure-matrix.mjs](../../../scripts/neutral-swarm/octahedral-coordinate-exposure-matrix.mjs) validates all nine affine columns by central finite differences. This resolves the derivative ambiguity between fixed-ledger exposure probes and true branch-coordinate motion.

The trace zero-mean route no longer uses the earlier $[0.5,1.5]$ diagnostic window as a theory constraint. [octahedral-bounded-speed-successor-closure-row](octahedral-bounded-speed-successor-closure-row.md) now records only a conditional declared-window lemma. [octahedral-period-rescaled-trace-scan](octahedral-period-rescaled-trace-scan.md) is the speed-unconstrained trace-period follow-on: in the ansatz $Y_i(u)=s\,p_i(u/h)$, the speed-ODE mean obeys $\mathcal M_i^\nu(s,h)=(1/h)C_i(s/h)$ on a fixed simple-root sheet. The sampled simple-root rows stay positive through $v=1.7$, and the first sampled one-root ledger failure appears at $v=1.75$. [octahedral-period-rescaled-fold-chart](octahedral-period-rescaled-fold-chart.md) sharpens that sampled boundary: the $\kappa=+1$ cross-binary row has a continuum saddle-node onset at $v_c\approx1.704939069887$. The $K_\varepsilon/L_J$ inlet now accepts the evaluator's certified branch-coordinate witness-set artifact directly when no external $M_K$ witness is supplied; the composer replays $K_\varepsilon$, extracts the generated $M_K$, and feeds it through the $L_J$ subset before the seven-component provenance replay. It can certify only the embedded h39 continuous-tail row; it does not trust prebuilt provenance, rejects fixed speed-band fields, and keeps scaled remainder, `I1`, quadrature, full primitive-vector status, and retained branch open.

H39 primitive slack update: the h39 root-tangent budget now inverts the Rouché-primitive scalar inequality into explicit strict tolerances for $E_R,\nu_J,L_J,\rho_X,r_X,M_G,$ and $M_R$. With $C_D=K_D/M_G-(40+1/(s-1))$, the core product condition is $(\nu_J-L_J\rho_X)(\rho_X-r_X)>M_R/C_D$, together with the existing strict Rouché graph-lift inequalities. This converts the remaining shared-domain backend from an open-ended search for acceptable primitive numbers into exact one-at-a-time directed-rounded margins, while still refusing retained h39 branch status until those values are certified on one graph-centered polydisc. The same reducer now accepts analytic-remainder allowances, forms the pessimistic $\rho_X,r_X$ rectangle, checks endpoint Rouché margins and the lower endpoint product, and reports whether the enlarged primitive tuple still satisfies the h39 scalar inequality. It also inverts a nonnegative analytic-remainder profile into a candidate maximum scale $\lambda_*^{\mathrm{safe}}$ using the monotone safe floor $J_{\mathrm{rob}}\sigma_{\mathrm{rob}}$, so the continuous backend can measure an entire remainder-pressure direction against one strict scalar margin. The shared-domain evaluator now consumes the known kernel-reduced $L_J$ pressure as such a profile direction and reports that the required scale $\lambda=1$ fits; the open row is therefore the still-missing $E_R,\nu_J,M_G,$ and $M_R$ analytic-remainder provenance, not another successor coefficient gate.

A fixed-radii subcase now has an exact primitive-profile boundary: simultaneous $E_R,\nu_J,L_J,M_G,$ and $M_R$ profile pressures fail first at the minimum of the $J_{\min}$ boundary, the graph Rouché-margin boundary, and the first positive root of the h39 scalar quadratic. The shared-domain evaluator also records the exact fixed-radii $\lambda=1$ headroom/deficit as a candidate success marker for the profile replay, while leaving the same directed-rounded provenance and retained-branch blockers unchanged.

H39 coordinate-source envelope update: the shared-domain evaluator now accepts ordered \texttt{coordinateSourceEnvelopeCandidates}. This lets a wide coordinate-source candidate fail with a recorded $\sinh$-majorant obstruction and then fall back to a smaller same-domain envelope without losing the cell. A full-cover h38 probe with coordinate source radii $0.01,0.01,0.02$ and denominator radius $0.01$ reports no source-certificate obstruction and keeps the upstream-source composer input-ready. The remaining blocker has moved from source-certificate existence to source sharpness: the certified small-radius route feeds an unshifted Cauchy pressure of order $10^{85}$ into $E_R$ and $M_R$ on the tested row, while larger radii soon overflow the coordinate $\sinh$ envelope. The next true h39 target is a shifted or cancellation-aware source envelope for $R_{\varepsilon,43}=F_\varepsilon/y^{43}$ and $y\,\partial_yR_{\varepsilon,43}$ on the same graph-centered domain.

H39 shifted-source envelope update: the shared-domain evaluator now has a distinct \texttt{shifted-removable-r43-cauchy-outer-bound} coordinate-source candidate. It consumes a direct shifted $R_{\varepsilon,43}$ outer bound plus a zero-prefix certificate for the removable quotient, verifies that the supplied bound dominates the shifted coefficient prefix, and feeds the primitive profile with Cauchy shift power $0$ rather than the raw $R^{-43}$ source division. A focused fixture with shifted bound $10^{-3}$ at radius $0.01$ reports $E_R\approx6.16\times10^{-7}$, $M_R\approx1.92\times10^{-7}$, and \texttt{h39-full-cauchy-primitive-profile-vector-candidate-closes}. The closure blocker is therefore narrowed again: certify a full-cover directed-rounded shifted $R_{\varepsilon,43}$ bound and zero-prefix witness on the same graph-centered domain, then feed that bound through the existing seven-input witness path.

H39 shifted-source full-cover diagnostic: a shifted-order $10$ scan at source radius $0.01$ reports maximum shifted-prefix pressure about $2.92\times10^{25}$ across the h38 cover, so a naive uniform shifted bound does not certify the live cover. Under-covering shifted candidates are now rejected before primitive-profile replay. The next true closure path is a sharper shifted-envelope proof, probably by certified subdomains or by another cancellation identity, not by deeper raw Cauchy prefixes.

H39 pressure-normal-form update: the evaluator now emits a shifted $R_{\varepsilon,43}$ pressure decomposition before summary aggregation. The full-cover diagnostic localizes the dominant pressure to `speed.0.first-y`, branch `-`, shifted index $k=0$: the total shifted-order $10$ pressure is about $2.9185\times10^{25}$, with about $2.8650\times10^{25}$ from the solved leading coefficient and about $5.35\times10^{23}$ left after removing that leading contribution. This changes the mathematical diagnosis. The largest obstruction is a graph-center correlation loss in the interval replay of the affine leading row, not a deep shifted-tail coefficient. A naive independent-interval Schur complement is not enough: on the same worst cell, the center-eliminated interval pressure can inflate to about $4.15\times10^{48}$. The next certificate route must preserve the $C_k,S_k,C_0,S_0$ correlations symbolically or by certified subdivision for shifted indices below the $y^{41}$ second-$X$ row, then separately bound the remaining center-eliminated coefficients and the $y^{41}$ second-$X$ remainder.

H39 numeric-center replay update: the shared-domain evaluator now preserves the solved center interval as a numeric interval for internal replay, instead of feeding the formatted display interval back into the centered branch inputs. A full-cover h38 probe at shifted order $10$, $\rho=0.001$, and source radius $0.01$ now certifies the affine-center leading zero on all $256$ branch rows. The raw shifted-prefix maximum remains about $2.87\times10^{25}$, but the affine-center finite-prefix maximum drops to about $5.25\times10^{22}$, with no open affine-zero rows. This proves the earlier $198$ open affine-zero rows were a center-interval replay artifact, not a geometric obstruction. The live obstruction has moved to `speed.0.first-y`, branch `-`, shifted index $1$ ($y$-order $44$), where the actual-center replay contributes about $5.24\times10^{22}$ at $\rho=0.001$ while independent Schur elimination for the same row is still about $4.06\times10^{47}$. The next certificate must therefore preserve the row-$1$ affine correlation, supply its shifted outer Cauchy tail, and add the separate $y^{41}K_\varepsilon$ second-$X$ remainder.

H39 row-$1$ correlation diagnostic update: the evaluator now has an opt-in `computeH39AffineCenterRowCorrelationDiagnosticCandidate` helper for a single shifted affine row. It compares full center-interval replay, uniform $X$-partition replay, center-midpoint replay, input-midpoint replay, and independent affine elimination while keeping all promotion flags false. On the live dominant row, a $2$-partition lowers the row pressure only from about $5.24\times10^{22}$ to about $3.78\times10^{22}$, and a $4$-partition lowers it to about $3.05\times10^{22}$; center-midpoint replay with the full h38 intervals is still about $1.83\times10^{22}$. When the h38 cell and inherited $h$ intervals are also collapsed to their midpoints, the row pressure drops to about $1.08\times10^{13}$, a factor of about $4.8\times10^9$. The row is therefore not merely a formatted-center artifact or shallow $X$-width artifact. The remaining pressure is mainly inherited h38 input-cell width in the current local coordinate, with a nearly tight source-term triangle and `sin_delta` the largest interval term contribution. The next advance should test certified h38 input subdivision or a stronger row-level identity before spending effort on a single global shifted outer envelope.

H39 h-row sensitivity update: grouped row-$1$ probes isolate the inherited $h$-coefficient widths as the main pressure source. Midpointing the live cell variables alone changes the row pressure by only a factor of about $1.000001$, and midpointing the inherited center-slope interval alone changes it by only about $1.00000023$. Midpointing all inherited $h$ intervals gives the factor $\approx4.8\times10^9$ collapse. Within the $h$ row, freezing $h_{38}$ alone gives only a factor $\approx2.86$, freezing $h_{37}\ldots h_{38}$ gives $\approx8.57$, freezing $h_{34}\ldots h_{38}$ gives $\approx184$, and freezing $h_{27}\ldots h_{38}$ gives $\approx7.2\times10^4$. The obstruction is therefore distributed inherited-coefficient width, not a single bad endpoint or speed-cell variable. A productive closure sprint should next build a certified h-row subdivision or dependency-preserving h-row transport certificate for the dominant row, rather than only further splitting $X$.

H39 h-row transport-depth update: `computeH39AffineCenterHRowSensitivityDiagnosticCandidate` now scans every suffix freeze from $h_{38}$ alone down through $h_0,\ldots,h_{38}$, not just the original hand-picked samples. On the cached live dominant row, the first $10$-fold, $10^2$-fold, $10^3$-fold, and $10^6$-fold pressure reductions occur after freezing $h_{36}\ldots h_{38}$, $h_{34}\ldots h_{38}$, $h_{32}\ldots h_{38}$, and $h_{20}\ldots h_{38}$ respectively. Freezing $h_{10}\ldots h_{38}$ already gives about $4.73\times10^9$, close to the full $h_0,\ldots,h_{38}$ midpoint factor $4.84\times10^9$. This makes the local no-go sharper: h38-only, h37/h38-only, or last-dozen-row repair cannot explain the full collapse. The closure route must transport a mid-chain predecessor recurrence dependency, or replace the exported independent h-row box by a lower-dimensional recurrence parameterization before applying the shifted source bound.

H39 live h-row diagnostic replay: on `speed.0.first-y`, branch `-`, the new helper reproduces the live pressure $5.239383640054425\times10^{22}$, h-row midpoint reduction $4.835860325251657\times10^9$, cell-only factor $1.0000010197237037$, slope-only factor $1.0000002309283613$, and best tested top-freeze factor $71897.68136115719$ for $h_{27}\ldots h_{38}$. The obstruction is therefore an executable same-domain h-row dependency problem, not an unlocalized envelope mystery.

H39 h-row width-compression update: the h-row diagnostic now also compresses all inherited $h$ intervals about their midpoints before re-solving the h39 center. On the live dominant row, compression factors $1,1/2,1/4,1/8,1/16,1/32$ give pressure ratios approximately $1,2,4,8,16,32$, while the exact midpoint replay gives the full $4.835860325251657\times10^9$ collapse. Thus the current independent h-row box behaves almost linearly in inherited h-width: brute independent subdivision would need about a $2^{-32}$ width scale before it resembles the midpoint replay, and doing that independently across many h-coordinates is not a practical closure route. The next proof should preserve the predecessor recurrence dependencies or transport a low-dimensional h-row parameterization into the H39 row, rather than trying to cover the exported $h_0,\ldots,h_{38}$ box directly.

H39 predecessor h-row provider-boundary update: `computeH39PredecessorHRowProviderBoundaryCandidate` now inspects an h38 row before H39 replay and distinguishes complete exported h-row interval snapshots from a true dependency-preserving h-row provider. The current h38 row boundary is complete as interval data but reports `independent-interval-snapshot-only`. The smallest evaluator entry point, `branchInputsFromH38Row`, now has an opt-in h-row provider hook. The hook rejects interval-only and flag-only providers, accepts only providers that carry derived h-intervals, a nonempty dependency trace, provider provenance, a dependency witness, a candidate-only claim boundary, and `preservesDependencies=true`, then surfaces a candidate-only `h_row_provider_report` in the evaluated cell and artifact summary. This still does not certify the shifted source; it creates the executable inlet through which a predecessor-recurrence transport or certified h-row subdivision witness can enter the row-$1$ replay without promoting shifted $R_{\varepsilon,43}$, shared-domain, continuous primitive, or retained-branch claims.

H39 embedded predecessor-provider update: the thirty-eighth-order post-$U$ successor certificate now emits dependency-preserving h-row provider metadata on each branch row. The metadata records the inherited $h_0,\ldots,h_{37}$ predecessor fields, the current $h_{38}$ recurrence solve, provider provenance, dependency trace, dependency witness, and a candidate-only claim boundary. The H39 evaluator consumes those embedded records without an external provider hook. A focused replay proves the important no-relief result: provider metadata over the same exported $h_0,\ldots,h_{38}$ interval box leaves the H39 row-$1$ $E_R,M_R$ finite-prefix pressure unchanged. The next closure move must therefore build a recurrence-refined subcover or a low-dimensional h-row transport, not merely attach provenance to the current independent h-row box.

H39 recurrence-refined predecessor-subcover update: the first actual transport experiment now works end to end at candidate level. The h24 producer accepts explicit speed samples, and the h25 through h38 successor builders can accept a supplied predecessor artifact, so a narrowed local chain can recompute $h_0,\ldots,h_{38}$ instead of only relabeling the exported box. On the live dominant `speed.0.first-y`, branch `-` row, bisecting the cell and replaying the refined h24-to-h38 chain lowers the H39 shifted-index-$1$ pressure from $5.239383640054425\times10^{22}$ to a local-subcover maximum about $2.6197784892373247\times10^{22}$, a factor $1.9999338347$ improvement. The local rows remain row-certified, but the artifact claim stays false because the subcover is not the standard full 128-cell h38 certificate. This is the first true pressure reduction from predecessor recurrence transport; by itself it also shows the remaining route is roughly width-linear and needs either many certified local subcells or a lower-dimensional recurrence parameterization to get the full $10^9$-scale collapse.

H39 recurrence-subcover scaling update: the local recurrence refinement is now a reusable diagnostic rather than a one-off test. `buildH39RecurrenceRefinedSubcoverPressureDiagnostic` runs the h24-to-h38 predecessor chain on selected local subcell counts and then replays the H39 shifted-index-$1$ source. On the dominant cell, $1,2,4$ local subcells give maximum pressures $5.239724324430226\times10^{22}$, $2.6197784892373247\times10^{22}$, and $1.3098669003931344\times10^{22}$, with coarse-to-refined ratios $1$, $2.0000638779$, and $4.0001959916$. The observed scaling exponent is $1.0000353436$, so brute subcovering would require about $4.83\times10^9$ local speed subcells to reach the h-row midpoint collapse. This rules out brute recurrence subdivision as the closure method and points to a lower-dimensional h-row transport normal form: carry the deterministic map $(\nu,\delta_f,\phi_f,\beta,\gamma,L,\varepsilon)\mapsto(h_0,\ldots,h_{38})$ into the H39 affine-center row so the exported independent h-row box is never formed.

H39 h-row width-normal-form update: the recurrence-subcover diagnostic now also measures the width scaling of each inherited $h_i$ interval. On the same $1,2,4$ local-subcell replay, all $39$ inherited h-row widths scale essentially linearly with local speed-cell width: the observed width exponents range from $0.9999948830$ to $1.0000300898$, with median $0.9999972227$. The gap between the H39 pressure exponent and the median h-width exponent is only about $3.8\times10^{-5}$. This makes the normal-form target more precise. The exported independent h-row box should be replaced by a single local recurrence-width parameter, plus smaller residual coordinates only if the next affine-transport proof needs them. The next proof object is not a deeper subcover but a same-domain affine/transport arithmetic witness for the map from the fold cell to the H39 affine-center row.

H39 one-noise affine h-row transport update: `buildH39OneNoiseAffineHRowTransportDiagnosticCandidate` now fits the line $h_i(\xi)=c_i+\xi d_i$ from the two refined H38 subcell midpoint rows, with $c_i=(h_i^-+h_i^+)/2$, $d_i=(h_i^+-h_i^-)/2$, and $\xi\in[-1,1]$. On the same explicit target interval `[3.02156, 3.02156007813]`, branch `-`, outer radius $0.001$, shifted index $1$, the independent h-row box pressure is $5.239724324430226\times10^{22}$, while the maximum sampled one-noise pressure is $6.978672999045361\times10^{12}$. The collapse factor is about $7.5081957919\times10^9$, and the one-noise maximum is within a factor $1.5541$ of the h-row midpoint replay. This changes the geometry diagnosis: the dangerous row is not merely width-linear under subdivision; it is already small on the recurrence transport line. The remaining blocker is to certify a same-domain enclosure around that one-noise affine graph, with residual directions if needed, rather than to subdivide the exported independent $h_0,\ldots,h_{38}$ box.

H39 affine graph subdivision update: `buildH39AffineHRowGraphSubdivisionDiagnosticCandidate` now replaces point samples by $\xi$-interval graph replay. The coarsest graph interval $\xi\in[-1,1]$ gives pressure $6.998682228480771\times10^{12}$, only about $1.0029$ times the worst sampled one-noise pressure, and an $8$-piece $\xi$ subdivision lowers the maximum to $6.981174153149333\times10^{12}$, only about $1.00036$ times the sampled maximum. Thus the affine graph itself is already interval-stable; the remaining obstruction is not nonlinear growth along $\xi$. The same diagnostic now also builds a provider-backed H39 shared-domain coefficient replay over the full $\xi$ graph interval, with `h_row_provider_dependency_state="dependency-preserving-provider-backed-replay"` and every closure flag still false. A producer residual check against $4$- and $8$-subcell H38 midpoint rows corrects the coverage claim: the affine graph alone is not yet a producer-image enclosure, with the largest residual at $h_{38}$. However, graph-plus-residual replay still has maximum pressure only $3.996425126569277\times10^{13}$, a factor $1.3111028378\times10^9$ below the independent h-row box. The certificate route is now sharper: certify the H38 producer image as an affine h-row graph plus bounded residual directions, then feed that graph-plus-residual provider into the H39 source replay without ever forming the independent $h_0,\ldots,h_{38}$ box.

H39 polynomial graph residual update: `buildH39PolynomialHRowGraphResidualDiagnosticCandidate` now tests whether the affine residual is low-degree producer curvature in the fold coordinate $\xi$ rather than an independent h-row noise box. On the dominant interval `[3.02156, 3.02156007813]`, branch `-`, it fits a quadratic graph $h_i(\xi)=\sum_{j=0}^2 a_{i,j}\xi^j$ from the four local H38 midpoint rows and replays the full fold coordinate range $\xi\in[-2,2]$. The best $8$-piece polynomial graph pressure is $5.043803779445108\times10^{12}$, about $1.04\times10^{10}$ below the independent h-row box. Against the $4$- and $8$-subcell producer midpoint checks, graph-plus-residual pressure falls to $1.5919512026398383\times10^{13}$, compared with the same-domain affine graph-plus-residual reference $3.9986141541875766\times10^{13}$. A cubic comparison gives essentially the same residual pressure, so the simpler quadratic graph is the current certificate target. The new diagnosis is that a substantial part of the residual is structural curvature of the predecessor recurrence image. The remaining blocker is not another h-row subdivision gate; it is a directed-rounded producer-image certificate for a polynomial h-row graph, plus a bounded residual term if the midpoint-refined producer rows do not exhaust the image.

H39 quadratic interval-residual update: `buildH39PolynomialHRowGraphIntervalResidualDiagnosticCandidate` replaces midpoint residual probes by actual H38 producer row interval residuals, computed as the producer $h_i$ interval minus the quadratic graph interval over the same $\xi$ subcell. This is the candidate form a directed-rounded H38 provider would have to export. The result is a controlled no-go for the naive interval route: graph-plus-interval-residual pressure is $1.3098672358675488\times10^{22}$, only a factor $4.000194967056995$ below the independent h-row box and $8.228061473840866\times10^8$ above the midpoint-residual replay. The dominant residual is still $h_{38}$; on the worst row its residual hull is about $\pm4.6388213974065\times10^{23}$, and the graph interval width is only about $2.0008\times10^{11}$, so the residual is essentially the H38 producer interval width. Doubling producer subcells from $4$ to $8$ halves the interval residual pressure with exponent $1.000013406731329$, implying about $3.290318928\times10^9$ local subcells to recover midpoint-scale pressure. The next closure route must therefore preserve more H38 recurrence dependency inside the residual itself, or use a piecewise/dependency-preserving residual normal form, rather than exporting a pure interval residual hull.

H39 correlated residual-width update: `buildH39CorrelatedResidualWidthDiagnosticCandidate` tests the next possible normal form, $h_i(\xi,\eta)=q_i(\xi)+c_i+r_i\eta$, where $[c_i-r_i,c_i+r_i]$ is the H38 producer interval residual hull. On the same dominant interval and shifted-index-$1$ replay, the maximum sampled correlated-width pressure is $3.7189936065423036\times10^{20}$. This is a factor $17.610343040598536$ below the interval-residual replay and a factor $140.89092046871806$ below the independent h-row box, so the residual width is partly low-dimensional and correlated. It is still $2.336754035638012\times10^7$ above the midpoint-residual replay; the midpoint-scale residual-noise fraction would have to be about $4.2805967717700346\times10^{-8}$. The $h_{38}$-only width replay already reaches $5.0026463677528906\times10^{20}$, while the full suffix only weakly cancels it. The obstruction is therefore not a generic residual-box artifact anymore; it is localized in the $h_{38}$ solve-width direction exported by the H38 producer. The next proof target is a dependency-preserving $h_{38}$ solve-width factorization, numerator/slope correlation certificate, or piecewise residual graph that shrinks the effective $\eta$ amplitude before H39 source replay.

H39 H38 solve-width factorization update: `buildH39H38SolveWidthFactorizationDiagnosticCandidate` splits the solved $h_{38}$ residual into recurrence numerator width and inherited solve-slope width before replaying the H39 shifted-index-$1$ source. The full $h_{38}$ solve-width replay is $5.002646364218093\times10^{20}$, while the numerator-only replay is $5.002646141988511\times10^{20}$, with pressure ratio $0.9999999555775951$. The slope-only replay collapses to $4.0904191135450396\times10^{12}$, essentially the both-midpoint replay $4.090419063566677\times10^{12}$. The solve-width ratios tell the same story: numerator-only width is $0.9999999555775948$ of full solve width, while slope-only width is only $9.990560893381585\times10^{-17}$ of full solve width and the quadratic graph interval width is only $2.1565763656489692\times10^{-13}$ of full solve width. The numerator midpoints themselves are smooth: a quadratic numerator graph has maximum midpoint residual $3.6011482546175\times10^{11}$ against maximum numerator interval width $3.67725335756\times10^{23}$, a ratio $9.793038184910384\times10^{-13}$. The obstruction is now localized more tightly: it is the H38 recurrence numerator interval before the divide, not the inherited slope interval or a generic numerator/slope quotient artifact. The next certificate route should factor, graph, or subdomain the H38 numerator residual itself before forming the solved $h_{38}$ interval.

H39 H38 numerator-graph solve update: `buildH39H38NumeratorGraphSolveDiagnosticCandidate` now moves the graph one level earlier, fitting $N_{38}$ before the solve $h_{38}=-N_{38}/S_{37}$ and then replaying the induced $h_{38}$ through H39. The numerator graph-only pressure is $4.0882852984579976\times10^{12}$, and the midpoint-residual numerator graph pressure is $4.0889087889467676\times10^{12}$, below the h-row midpoint replay by a factor $0.3770263622171746$. Keeping the full slope interval changes the graph-only pressure by only $1.000000831612749$, and the slope-induced graph width is only $7.989590547586257\times10^{-16}$ of the full solve width. By contrast, replacing the small numerator residual by the ordinary numerator interval-residual hull raises pressure to $5.002660154369953\times10^{20}$, a factor $1.223470713725202\times10^8$ above the correlated midpoint-residual graph and essentially equal to the full solve-width obstruction. This is a controlled no-go for a raw interval hull on $N_{38}$ and a positive route for a dependency-preserving numerator graph or Taylor enclosure.

H39 $N_{38}$ residual-budget update: `buildH39H38NumeratorGraphResidualBudgetDiagnosticCandidate` now quantifies how sharp the dependency-preserving $N_{38}$ enclosure must be before H39 replay returns to h-row-midpoint scale. Across $4$, $8$, and $16$ local producer subcells, the raw numerator interval-residual pressure scales almost exactly linearly with subcell width: exponent $1.0000016614193274$ for pressure and $1.0000016681321142$ for residual width. The raw residual hull remains essentially the whole numerator interval width at every scale, while the midpoint residual width is already far below the budget. The worst h-row-midpoint-scale budget requires shrinking the raw numerator residual by a factor $1.7245027326360488\times10^8$; the midpoint residual width is at most $0.00011864454815927154$ of the allowed budget. This is the first concrete Taylor-enclosure target: the $N_{38}$ provider does not need exact midpoint cancellation, but it must prove a dependency-preserving remainder roughly eight orders below the raw interval hull before forming $h_{38}=-N_{38}/S_{37}$.

H39 local $N_{38}$ partition update: `buildH39H38NumeratorGraphLocalPartitionDiagnosticCandidate` tests whether the $N_{38}$ graph should be certified as a local Taylor partition rather than a single global graph. Using a $16$-subcell H38 producer cover and local quadratic fits over $1$, $2$, and $4$ $\xi$ partitions, the midpoint-residual H39 replay stays below the h-row-midpoint target in every case; the best ratio is $0.3308706978209664$. But the raw row-field interval hull remains unchanged in kind: the best raw-hull pressure is still about $2.5013444686\times10^{20}$, a factor $6.970749744386734\times10^7$ above the best midpoint-residual replay. Local partitioning of exported row fields therefore does not recover the needed Taylor remainder. The next certificate must evaluate the H38 recurrence numerator expression itself, namely the `sourceEquationSeries` coefficient used for `h38_residual_before_solve`, and prove a directed-rounded local Taylor remainder before the numerator is exported as an interval hull.

H39 expression-level $N_{38}$ Taylor-budget update: `buildH39H38ExpressionN38TaylorBudgetDiagnosticCandidate` now quantifies the local normal-form route after the expression-level numerator helper. On the live dominant row, the one-piece raw expression interval has width $2.9419491126351116\times10^{24}$ while the point-local term-width scale is $236$; with observed width-linear exponent $1.000023935850454$, a uniform subcover would need about $1.245071482894444\times10^{22}$ local cells to reach point scale. The one-piece width decomposes into $48.6117523761\%$ $\sin\delta$, $35.9913177414\%$ $\sin\phi$, $15.3969298825\%$ $\delta^2/\nu^2$, and zero constant contribution. A cubic point-midpoint fit for the direct expression has residual $4.71369097121875\times10^9$, only $1.6022340260660338\times10^{-15}$ of the raw interval width, but still too large to be a certificate by itself. Interpreting that residual only as a fourth-order local Taylor-scaling proxy gives a finite directed-rounded tiling target: about $67$ local $\xi$ tiles for the direct expression, $93$ for $\sin\phi$, and $86$ for $\sin\delta$. The obstruction is therefore a dependency-loss artifact localized to the sine-bearing expression-level numerator; the next true blocker is a directed-rounded Taylor or normal-form enclosure that keeps shared $\delta,\phi$ dependency before the H38 solve interval is formed.

H39 expression-level $N_{38}$ Taylor-enclosure prototype update: `buildH39H38ExpressionN38TaylorEnclosurePrototypeCandidate` turns the local Taylor-budget estimate into explicit finite rows. For the direct expression, $\sin\phi$, and $\sin\delta$, it emits $246$ component-tile rows with maximum tile count $93$. Under the candidate fourth-order scaling law, the inflated prototype remainders are $233.91705956302627$, $232.1086233629665$, and $228.95202748895008$, respectively, all below the point-local term-width scale $236$. The brute-to-prototype tile-count ratio is about $1.3387865407467138\times10^{20}$. This is not a directed-rounded Taylor certificate; it is the finite same-domain derivative-bound target that should replace the raw row-hull subcover path.

H39 expression-level $N_{38}$ Taylor derivative-bound prototype update: `buildH39H38ExpressionN38TaylorDerivativeBoundPrototypeCandidate` now converts those finite tile rows into the explicit fourth-derivative inequalities a certificate must prove. The current H39 closure sprint has converted the full-cover shifted
$R_{\varepsilon,43}$ obstruction from a raw prefix-size problem into a
certified affine-center replay problem. The evaluator now carries an
`R43_affine_center_form_candidate` that preserves the center solve
$X_c=-C_0/S_0$, zeroes only the certified leading affine coefficient, and
keeps independent Schur products out of the finite-prefix bound. After the
numeric-center replay correction, all $256$ live h38 branch rows certify that
leading affine zero. The remaining geometry-bridge task is now sharper: the
dominant finite-prefix row is shifted index $1$ at $y$-order $44$, so the
evaluator can form an affine-center shifted source profile, leave it diagnostic
without a shifted outer Cauchy pair, and attach a shift-power-zero tail only
when that outer pair is supplied on the same domain. The second-$X$
contribution is recorded separately as the $y^{41}K_\varepsilon$ remainder and
is not included in $E_R,M_R$ until its own graph-centered data are certified.
The next proof burden is therefore a directed-rounded same-domain
affine-center shifted outer bound for the shifted-index-$1$ replay coefficient
and its tail, plus the separate second-$X$ remainder, not another reducer
interface or fixed speed-band assumption.
The h-row sensitivity route is no longer only an interpretation: the evaluator
now has an executable candidate-only h-row diagnostic that re-solves the center
after selected midpoint families and top-h-row freezes. It leaves the live
closure claims open, but it turns the next sprint into a precise predecessor
h-row transport/subdivision proof problem instead of another blind prefix
search.
The latest transport diagnostic advances that proof problem again: the live

coordinate $\xi$ than by an affine graph plus large independent residual
directions. The obstruction is therefore partly a bounding-coordinate artifact
introduced when the H38 recurrence image is exported as an independent h-row
box. H39 closure still needs directed-rounded producer-image enclosure, but the
candidate target has narrowed from "cover many independent h intervals" to
"certify a low-degree graph image plus a small residual."

blocker: the allowed $u$ half-width for the center-sample pressure scale is

certificate route is therefore not another raw prefix search; it is a
directed-rounded H38 producer-image certificate that preserves this signed
affine $h_{38}$ relation, or a covariance-preserving H39 source replay that
removes the $8.5068\times$ interval amplification before the shifted
$R_{\varepsilon,43}$ bound is promoted. A follow-on replay divides the
zero-centered half-widths by the observed amplification and brings the nonzero
reference ladder to maximum over-target ratio $1.0000057252904435$. That is
still candidate-only, but it shows the remaining gap is now a small directed

safety search now tightens this to an executable candidate divisor:
$8.507854082824874$, only $1.0001255923907613$ times the observed maximum
amplification. The existing center-eliminated affine-row replay does not solve
the problem; it inflates to maximum over-target ratio
$1.3394690276662064\times10^{24}$. The next proof target is therefore a
same-domain signed-affine H38 producer-image certificate with this safety
divisor, or a stronger covariance-preserving replay that avoids the divisor.
The current H39/y44 product-segment replay has now ruled out a denominator

The whole-collar finite-polynomial target now has an outward interval replay.

The same replay now emits a true-stream slack budget. After subtracting the

The slack budget is now emitted as an excess target against the directed

The follow-on provider probe rules out two tempting shortcuts. If the

The signed point/drift collar provider probe now tests that route directly in

The paired-subcell signed provider is the next executable refinement of that

The direct-collapse locality trace now resolves what kind of dependency remains.

The same-variable direct normal-form candidate now tests that proof object at

The producer-interval direct replay now separates coverage from size. It
evaluates the same finite-polynomial direct residual-derivative model on the
actual H38 producer $\xi$ intervals and recomputes the Lagrange-product
derivative guard on those same intervals. The diagnostic status is
`positive-n38-producer-interval-direct-normal-form-diagnostic-denominator-guard-open`:
rows `speed.1.first-y` and `speed.3.first-y` have separated product
derivative and stay inside headroom, with maximum guarded ratio
$5.425381767312044\times10^{-7}$, minimum product-derivative lower bound
$0.7692446997498641$, minimum slack ratio $0.9999994574618234$, and minimum
excess-to-directed ratio $1.843187263773447\times10^6$. Rows
`speed.0.first-y`, `speed.2.first-y`, and `speed.4.first-y` are not size
failures; their full producer intervals cross a zero of the Lagrange-product
derivative. The next closure move is therefore a product-derivative-aware
producer partition, or an equivalent local coordinate that avoids using a
repeated-node quotient across those zero crossings.

The hybrid quotient replay now resolves that finite-polynomial support problem
at candidate level. It uses the repeated-node derivative quotient where
$P'(\xi)$ is separated and switches to the product quotient on producer
complements where $P(\xi)$ is separated. The live H38 producer image is covered
by nine segments: five product-quotient segments and four derivative-quotient
segments. All nine stay inside headroom, with maximum segment ratio
$7.342231140809408\times10^{-7}$, minimum denominator lower bound
$0.7692446997498641$, minimum slack ratio $0.999999265776886$, and minimum
excess-to-directed ratio $1.3619828177551026\times10^6$. This does not certify
the true stream or H39 shifted closure; it shows that the previous open rows
were a quotient-coordinate denominator artifact at the finite-polynomial level.
The next blocker is a directed-rounded true-stream residual and derivative
enclosure on this same hybrid producer partition, followed by
dependency-preserving $S_{37}$ division and signed H39 shifted-source replay.

The hybrid partition now carries that next proof target as an executable
excess-budget diagnostic. The provider target emits nine segment-wise

same-variable direct residual enclosure, and four derivative-quotient segments
require a same-variable direct residual-derivative enclosure. Every segment has

same maximum finite-model ratio $7.342231140809408\times10^{-7}$, minimum
slack ratio $0.999999265776886$, and minimum excess-to-directed ratio

true-stream excess budget $1.921420936834246\times10^{14}$ after the

remaining closure burden is now a finite list of same-domain residual and

partition.

The split-stream paired replay now tests the signed point-plus-drift streams
on that same nine-segment hybrid partition. It evaluates the finite-polynomial
point-expression residual and interval-center drift residual together on each

All nine segments stay inside their targets, with maximum paired-to-target
ratio $1.318673422133216\times10^{-6}$, maximum split-triangle-to-target ratio
$1.5966996776715186\times10^{-5}$, minimum paired slack ratio

The same provider target now also carries a raw true-source replay on the

Two follow-on provider replays now locate the dependency loss more sharply. A
polynomial h-row graph plus per-h interval residual hull preserves the shared

Subdividing the H38 residual coordinate into $16$ subcells leaves that factor

The next H39/y44 replay rejects both of those first candidate explanations.
Giving selected non-H38 residual rows the same normalized coordinate as

That residual inequality has now been tightened to a direct-quartic alignment
no-go for the current H38-coordinate provider. On the controlling segment, the

The full residual-vector provider no-go has now sharpened this again. Fitting

$5.724943127080209\times10^{-4}$; the full-vector/H38-coordinate offset ratio

The solve/divisor-normalization hypothesis is now an executable no-go. The

The source-pair split now identifies that numerator normal form more precisely.

The quotient refinement ladder now shows why the provider must stay split by

The cell-bearing packaging probe crosses the next executable boundary. Each

replays the h20-through-h38 requested shifted-$1$, $y^{44}$ coefficient over the

The diagnostic records that the row intervals cover the comparison $\xi$ hull,
so this is no longer only a midpoint-stencil observation. Every interval row
still covers the fixed requested coefficient before taking the maximum, every

below $1.001$. Thus the obstruction is not a narrow $\xi$ endpoint spike to hunt

residual-direction wall: $\zeta=-1$ and $\zeta=1$ are `sin_delta` dominated,
while the midpoint $\zeta=0$ is `sin_phi` dominated and much smaller. The next
certificate must promote this same row-interval $\xi,\zeta$ cover into a
directed-rounded producer-image enclosure for that fixed requested coefficient.

H39 requested-coefficient source-term normal-form update: the same route now
records the signed source-term decomposition for the fixed requested shifted-$1$,

On the live h20-through-h38 affine-floor cover, the four source terms reconstruct

H39 requested-coefficient residual-direction profile update: the h20 affine-floor
route now collapses each row's duplicate $\zeta$ boundary endpoints into a

H39 requested-coefficient residual-radius budget update: the residual-direction

The producer-image budget comparison now checks that target against the existing

The row-local producer-budget refinement sharpens that conclusion. The H39

The row-local producer-collar replay tightens the numerical target again. The

The row-local collar bisection turns the estimate into an executable target.

The row-local producer-width contraction packet converts that collar into H38

$7.308309016282101\times10^{16}$ and a pre-division numerator-width lower

The row-local $N_{38}/S_{37}$ collar diagnostic now tests the same target one

The same row-local diagnostic now records the exact acceptance inequality for
that next certificate:
$w(\mathrm{graph})+2|r_{\mathrm{mid}}|+w(r_{\mathrm{sd}})\le
w_{S_{37}}^{\mathrm{row}}$. On the live selected rows, the graph-plus-midpoint
part is below $10^{-9}$ of the row-local $S_{37}$ numerator-width target, so

The row-local route now also attaches that inequality to the true H38
producer stream. For the cubic Taylor graph $q_3$, the fourth derivative of

The first analytic provider scaffold is now explicit. The certificate may
commute coefficient extraction with the row coordinate derivative:
$d^4[y^{42}]S(y,\xi)/d\xi^4=[y^{42}]d^4S(y,\xi)/d\xi^4$ on the same
$\xi$ domain, where
$S=\delta^2\nu^{-2}-2+\sin\phi+\sin\delta$ and $h_{38}$ is zeroed before the
solve. Thus the missing directed-rounded inputs are no longer vague: they are
the $\xi$ derivatives through order $4$ of $\nu^{-2}$, of the $\delta$
coefficient stream, and of the $\phi$ coefficient stream, inserted into the
Leibniz formula for $\delta^2\nu^{-2}$ and the fourth-order sine chain rule for
$\sin\phi$ and $\sin\delta$. This remains candidate-only, but it turns the next
certificate from "differentiate the evaluator somehow" into a concrete
chain-rule coefficient-extraction provider. The executable route now includes a
standalone scaffold candidate that accepts same-row source-term
$d^4/d\xi^4$ intervals, preserves the signed sum of
$\delta^2\nu^{-2}$, $\sin\phi$, and $\sin\delta$ through the provider boundary,
and compares the resulting $N_{38}^{(4)}$ upper bound with the row-local $M_4$
ceiling. It still marks the result non-certifying unless the supplied term
intervals are themselves directed-rounded same-domain enclosures.

The follow-on normal-form check now separates the finite-stencil number from
the interpolation identity it represents. On the same five live producer nodes,
the diagnostic forms a degree-four Lagrange interpolant for each source-term
midpoint stream and takes the fourth derivative as $24$ times the quartic
coefficient. The summed interpolant derivative fits the row-local $M_4$ ceiling
by a factor below $5.49\times10^{-11}$, and the interpolant-vs-fourth-divided
difference gap is below $8.45\times10^{-20}$ of that ceiling. This is still

intervals replacing midpoint interpolants.

The intervalized follow-up now tests whether the existing node coefficient
intervals are already tight enough for that replacement. The diagnostic carries

$3.26\times10^4$ for $\delta^2\nu^{-2}$, $7.62\times10^4$ for $\sin\phi$,
and $1.03\times10^5$ for $\sin\delta$. This localizes the obstruction: the
Lagrange normal form is not failing, and the midpoint source-term cancellation
is present inside the interval hull; the remaining blocker is dependency loss
in the node source-term intervals before a directed-rounded same-domain
derivative certificate is applied.

The next provider probe removes one more possible artifact. Instead of summing

$5.88\times10^{23}$ wide, so the obstruction is present inside the H38
producer node intervals themselves, not only in the termwise source split. The
next certificate must therefore preserve dependency while forming the node
$N_{38}$ interval or prove a sharper same-domain continuous derivative
enclosure before Lagrange weights and absolute Cauchy bounds are applied.

The node-width localizer now identifies where that total-$N_{38}$ interval
width is born. For the live selected H39/y44 rows, pinning all h rows to their
midpoints and leaving only the cell geometry interval active leaves only about
$2.65\times10^{-15}$ of the full node width. Keeping the cell at its midpoint
while restoring all h-row intervals recovers essentially all of the width
($0.999999876$ of the full node width). The late terminal rows dominate that

the nonterminal h0-h34 block accounts for only about $0.037012$. Among single
terminal rows, h37 is the largest contributor at about $0.649856$ of the full
width. This changes the immediate closure target: the obstruction is not
ordinary cell-geometry uncertainty and not the Lagrange provider. It is a late
h-row dependency-width problem in the H38 producer image, so the next proof
route should preserve covariance through h35-h37, or derive a terminal h-row
normal form that avoids exporting those rows as independent intervals before
the $N_{38}$ derivative certificate.

The terminal eta graph replay now bridges that localizer back onto the exact

The shared-domain replay summary now records the provider dependency kind,

The terminal graph remainder-budget diagnostic now carries the same symmetric
cross-fold row-local window: the affine endpoint partitions remain under the

The H39/y44 route now preserves the requested shifted residual-direction
profile through that coupled provider summary and emits a checked

The same target now carries a linear width-share budget and a provider-backed
endpoint-partition witness. The width-share budget shows that terminal h35-h37
dominance is real but terminal-only compression cannot close the selected rows
if the nonterminal h0-h34 floor is held fixed: the nonterminal-plus-cell floor
is already about $7.84\times10^3$ times the required closure share, while the

`candidate-requested-y44-terminal-h35-h37-coupled-endpoint-partition-target`
then proves the route can carry the exact `[37,36,35]` terminal endpoint
provider, requested shifted $\zeta$-slice coverage, and source reconstruction

preserve a broader all-h-row covariance through the H38 producer image or prove

majorant is applied.

The same replay now makes that conclusion quantitative as a two-block
allocation simplex. With the cell floor removed, the selected rows need the

$7.84\times10^3$ before any positive terminal h35-h37 budget exists. Even if

it changes the closure route from "certify h35-h37 alone" to "certify an
all-h-row covariance enclosure or prove signed h0-h34 cancellation before the
absolute Cauchy majorant."

The replay also now carries a
`candidate-signed-nonterminal-h0-h34-floor-cancellation-target` on the same
rows. This reuses the h0-h34 cell-midpoint localization intervals and the

The live routed numbers now choose that fork. On the selected rows, the
h0-h34 intervalized floor is about $7.84\times10^3$ times the row-local

That identity target is now explicit. The replay adds
`candidate-signed-nonterminal-h0-h34-midpoint-fourth-difference-identity-target`,
which applies the same five-node degree-four Lagrange weights directly to the

`signed-nonterminal-fourth-difference-cancels-before-interval-floor`: the
signed-to-absolute midpoint ratio is about $1.70\times10^{-6}$, the

only about $3.93\times10^{-4}$ of the row-local requirement. The nonterminal
obstruction is therefore not a large midpoint fourth difference. It is a

covariance target forward.

The follow-on budget now measures that dependency loss directly. The
`candidate-signed-nonterminal-h0-h34-dependency-loss-budget` compares the
intervalized h0-h34 floor with both the signed midpoint fourth difference and
the absolute midpoint mass. The live rows show that the absolute midpoint mass
already has about $2.54\times10^3$ headroom against the row-local requirement,
while the interval floor is about $2.0\times10^7$ times the absolute midpoint
mass and about $1.17\times10^{13}$ times the signed midpoint. Thus the next
certificate is not searching for more cancellation at the midpoint. It must

budget now subtracts the midpoint contribution from the intervalized
fourth-difference interval and records the midpoint-centered residual burden;

charged.

The next proof object is now executable as a provider specification instead of
a vague enclosure request. The
`candidate-signed-nonterminal-h0-h34-midpoint-dependency-preserving-provider-specification-target`
converts the midpoint-centered residual budget into five coefficient-residual
radius targets, one for each Lagrange node. It records the residual derivative
budget left after charging the absolute midpoint mass, divides that budget
across the five weighted nodes, and solves the local inequality
$|w_i|\,\varepsilon_i \le B_i$ for the coefficient residual radius
$\varepsilon_i$. It also records the dominant node for the required radius
compression, so the next proof attempt can focus on the hardest local residual
instead of re-running an undirected h0-h34 search. The live rows still require
directed-rounded radius contraction; the current intervals do not satisfy the
specification. This is the sharper closure route: construct a same-domain
h0-h34 provider whose node residual radii meet those bounds, then replay the
remaining terminal/all-h-row covariance before returning to shifted
$R_{\varepsilon,43}$.

The provider specification now has a current-shape comparison. The
`candidate-signed-nonterminal-h0-h34-current-shape-radius-contraction-profile-target`
compares the balanced per-node residual allocation with the minimax allocation
that preserves the live five-node residual shape. If $B$ is the total residual
derivative budget and $d_i$ is the current weighted residual magnitude at node
$i$, the optimized allocation is $B_i=B\,d_i/\sum_j d_j$. Under that
allocation every active node has the same required contraction,
$\sum_i d_i/B$. This exposes whether the hardest balanced node is a real
residual obstruction or an artifact of splitting the budget equally. On the
live rows the current-shape profile sharpens the balanced radius target, so the
next directed-rounded h0-h34 provider should try to contract the existing
five-node residual shape uniformly around the midpoint stream rather than
forcing an equal-budget node proof first.

The profile now feeds an executable candidate certificate screen rather than a
pure description of the next contraction. The
`candidate-signed-nonterminal-h0-h34-current-shape-certificate-screen` keeps the
same five-node weighted residual shares and tests explicit safety margins
against the remaining residual derivative budget. At the boundary contraction
factor the contracted current-shape residual exactly fits the h0-h34 budget; at
the first strict margin, $1.01$ times that factor, the screen reports positive
headroom while preserving the node-share sum. This still does not certify
directed rounding or h0-h34 covariance, but it turns the next provider proof
into a concrete contract: prove each midpoint-centered node residual contracts
by at least the selected current-shape factor, then consume the resulting
budget-fitting h0-h34 provider before replaying terminal/all-h-row covariance.

The screened-provider gap has now collapsed to one scalar target. The
`candidate-signed-nonterminal-h0-h34-uniform-provider-gap-target` compares the
live node residual radii to the first strict screened radii and verifies that

spread ratio is one, so the next h0-h34 proof does not need a separate
node-specific allocation theorem. It needs a directed-rounded same-domain proof
that the midpoint-centered current residual shape contracts by that single
factor while preserving the five-node dependency.

The follow-on
`candidate-signed-nonterminal-h0-h34-single-contraction-provider-contract`
turns that statement into an interval-inclusion contract. For each interpolation
node, the screened zero-centered target interval is a strict subinterval of the

remaining proof burden is now sharply stated: a directed-rounded same-domain
h0-h34 residual evaluator must emit intervals contained in those screened
targets before the terminal/all-h-row replay can consume the contracted
nonterminal block.

The provider-verifier boundary is now executable rather than prose-only. The
`candidate-signed-nonterminal-h0-h34-directed-provider-verifier` consumes the
single-contraction contract and checks the exact row shape that a future
directed-rounded provider must supply: five h0-h34 rows on the same nodes,
same active range, same midpoint-centered target radii, and directed-rounded
weighted plus coefficient residual intervals contained in the screened targets.
With no true provider rows supplied, the verifier runs only a screened-target
fixture path; it deliberately leaves same-domain, h0-h34 covariance, shifted
$R_{\varepsilon,43}$, and retained-branch claims false. This advances the
blocker from "what provider should exist?" to "produce real directed-rounded
h0-h34 rows that pass the already wired inclusion verifier."

The verifier now also consumes the live provider-shaped residual rows as a
fail-closed probe. The
`candidate-signed-nonterminal-h0-h34-live-residual-provider-probe` copies the
existing midpoint-centered weighted residual intervals and coefficient residual
intervals into the provider-row shape and feeds them through the verifier. The
probe reaches the same-domain and same-radius contract, but it is explicitly
not directed-rounded evidence and its live intervals are wider than the
screened targets. This is useful mathematically because the blocker is no
longer a missing interface: it is a measured contraction/certification problem
on already identified live residual intervals.

The follow-on
`candidate-signed-nonterminal-h0-h34-live-provider-contraction-defect` measures
that problem directly at the verifier boundary. It compares the live weighted
and coefficient residual intervals with the screened target intervals and
records the required live-to-screened contraction for each node and residual
view. The current live defect reduces to the same single uniform contraction
factor already selected by the upstream contract; no node-specific or
view-specific geometry mismatch appears at this stage. The remaining blocker is
therefore sharper: produce a same-domain directed-rounded h0-h34 provider that
realizes this single contraction factor and carries proof-grade source
provenance, then let the existing verifier consume it.

The contracted-provider candidate now executes that verifier-side split. The
`candidate-signed-nonterminal-h0-h34-contracted-provider-candidate` feeds the
five single-contraction h0-h34 target intervals back through the existing
directed-provider verifier as supplied provider rows. Those rows match the same
domain and same target radii and are contained in the screened targets; the
verifier still rejects them because their source kind is candidate-only and
does not certify directed-rounded shared-domain provenance. This is a real
narrowing of the blocker: interval containment is no longer the candidate-level
obstruction. The remaining h0-h34 proof burden is source certification for the
same five contracted weighted and coefficient residual intervals.

The scaled live-source diagnostic sharpens that burden by testing the actual
arithmetic map instead of only supplying the screened targets. The
`candidate-signed-nonterminal-h0-h34-scaled-live-source-provider-diagnostic`
divides each live midpoint-centered h0-h34 residual interval by the measured
single contraction factor and replays the scaled rows through the same verifier.
The scaled intervals match the screened targets to tolerance, but strict
containment misses by only a floating directed-rounding margin: the largest
relative endpoint gap is about $2.03\times10^{-16}$. The next proof object is
therefore not a new h0-h34 geometry search. It is a directed-rounded
same-domain source map for the existing h0-h34-cell-midpoint evaluator stream,
with enough outward-margin control to turn the scaled arithmetic replay into a
real provider certificate.

The source-map diagnostic now makes that localization executable. The
`candidate-signed-nonterminal-h0-h34-source-map-diagnostic` ties each scaled
provider row back to the h0-h34-cell-midpoint stream by checking the coefficient
residual, Lagrange-weighted residual, live contract row, and uniform scaled row
identities before the verifier sees the absolute intervals. On the live H39
route these arithmetic identities close with maximum relative gap about
$2.10\times10^{-16}$, while the scaled row to screened target gap remains about
$2.03\times10^{-16}$. The diagnostic still leaves all directed-rounded,
covariance, retained-branch, and shifted $R_{\varepsilon,43}$ claims false; it
only proves that the remaining h0-h34 obstruction is the directed-rounded
source operation and endpoint margin, not an unknown source row mismatch.

The endpoint-margin diagnostic now quantifies that last numerical edge. The
`candidate-signed-nonterminal-h0-h34-source-map-endpoint-margin-diagnostic`
computes, for each source-map row, the smallest relative outward endpoint
margin needed for the screened target interval to contain the scaled h0-h34
source-map interval. On the live H39 route a single `Number.EPSILON` relative
endpoint expansion contains every weighted and coefficient interval. This does
not certify directed rounding; it narrows the next certificate to proving that
the h0-h34-cell-midpoint source operation may carry that explicit one-epsilon
outward endpoint margin on the same domain.

The source-input provenance diagnostic identifies the exact live input path
feeding that operation. The
`candidate-signed-nonterminal-h0-h34-source-input-provenance-diagnostic`
checks that each of the five source rows comes from the same-row
`h0-h34-cell-midpoint` localization path: the cell is replaced by

`evaluateH38RecurrenceNumeratorBeforeSolve` supplies the $N_{38}$ coefficient
interval. This does not certify the producer image as directed-rounded. It
does remove the ambiguity about what source operation must be certified next:
the remaining object is a same-row active-h0-h34 input certificate with h35-h38
midpoint freezing, not an independent h-row box or an unknown provider mismatch.

The same-row source-input replay diagnostic now checks that the stored h0-h34
source intervals are reproducible from those original row inputs. The
`candidate-signed-nonterminal-h0-h34-source-input-replay-diagnostic` rebuilds
the `h0-h34-cell-midpoint` evaluator call from the row cell, branch, and

by `evaluateH38RecurrenceNumeratorBeforeSolve`, and compares the replayed

route the replay closes with sub-$10^{-12}$ relative endpoint gap. This is a
provenance/regression certificate, not a directed-rounded producer-image
certificate; it proves the remaining blocker is source trust, not a stored-row
bookkeeping mismatch.

The source-operation replay layer separates the machine arithmetic from the
source-provenance problem. The
`candidate-signed-nonterminal-h0-h34-source-operation-replay` recomputes the
coefficient-midpoint subtraction, Lagrange weighting, and single-factor scaling

live H39 rows the binary64 outward operation path closes with sub-$10^{-12}$
relative replay gaps and every operation row fits the endpoint-margin target.
The artifact keeps source-input, covariance, shifted $R_{\varepsilon,43}$,
directed-rounded, and retained-branch claims false; it reduces the live blocker
to proof-grade provenance for the five h0-h34 coefficient intervals and
midpoint subtractions feeding the already-closed operation replay.

The source-trust gap diagnostic now pins that handoff to the replay chain. The
`candidate-signed-nonterminal-h0-h34-source-trust-gap-diagnostic` starts from

The source-term producer-image trace pushes the same localization one level

`candidate-signed-nonterminal-h0-h34-source-term-producer-image-trace` keeps

The zero-constant source-term reduction removes one of those four terms from

The same three-term boundary is now replayed at the aggregate H39 producer-image

The signed nonconstant source-sum bridge now rules out one more possible

`row_local_n38_nonconstant_source_sum_xi_intervalized_lagrange_provider_candidate`

`buildH39RequestedY44RowLocalN38SignedSourceSumDerivativeProviderCandidate`.
On the live selected rows, the maximum ratio is still

The signed-source derivative allocation target now makes that proof object

The source-map residual covariance target now moves that diagnosis one layer

The requested-y44 target is now linked back to the existing h0-h34 source-map

The requested-y44 source-map target now reduces the selected-row proof burden

The same five-node source-map target now has a provider-boundary verifier.

The requested-y44 route now has an executable $N_{38}$ source-map envelope

The next executable step now exists as a candidate terminal-affine endpoint

## Current Classification

This folder is `priority-only`. It should not be linked from `content/markdown/aaa` until at least one theorem-target row is promoted into a reader-facing corpus file. It may link to corpus and priority files as needed for workstream coordination.
